From my point of view, this is in a tension ratio: On the one hand, the process economy is very important, but on the other hand, we do not want to see what is often referred to as American relations in the sense of Class Action; of course, this also has its disadvantages, one has to say quite clearly. However, for individuals affected by a problem that affects many others, the enforcement of law should be simplified in the sense of procedural economy, but also in the sense that there is no risk that something will delay, just because someone as a single person may be more difficult to pursue his right. That's the tension ratio in which we move. We will now again be able to devote ourselves more fully to this issue within the framework of the working group, which exists in the Federal Ministry of Justice, in which all the stakeholders who have something to say in this regard can be involved – and work will progress as always with us.

Some of my colleagues have referred to this, and it may well be that this concept will also be a model at European level. This model is absolutely compatible with all the requirements that have arisen at the level of the European Court of Justice. I think it's also quite fine if one can fix a longer storage time due to a initial suspicion – i.e., cause-related, not cause-less – and then look for a reasonable period of time to see if the suspicion of crime is concrete. Then you can access the data with judicial control and legal certainty. This is exactly the model we need, and it is reasonable to have nothing against it.

Secondly, the legal adult representation by a close relative. This also does not happen directly through law, but only if you are registered in the Central Office register. Here, in addition, judicial control is provided for after three years. The chosen adult representation is completely new, i.e. you can choose a representative yourself as a victim. There is also a corresponding judicial control, and this is also applicable only if one is entered in the list of representations. Then there is the attraction of the precautionary power, which offers the possibility that, when the precautionary event occurs, one may have a representative. ParlaMint-AT_2017-03-30-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00173_d7e7284	That is why we now come to points 2 and 3 of the agenda on which the debate is being held under one. There was no oral reporting. ParlaMint-AT_2017-03-30-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00173_d7e7315	Mr President. Minister! My dear! Ladies and gentlemen! Dear listeners! Dear listeners! We are now discussing the amendment of antitrust and competition law. Cartels are the merger, the cooperation of economic units, which, if not properly made, can cause problems. It may result in unauthorised price agreements, and this may lead to the disadvantage of other market participants and consumers. These disadvantages are to be overcome as much as possible, and there is an EU directive to be implemented here. She'd be up to 16 years old. In December of the previous year, parts of this law will therefore also become effective at this time. This Directive mainly codifies the case law of the European Court of Justice and, above all, requires full compensation. What do you understand? – That all directly injured, but also all indirectly injured, are entitled to compensation for damages. That is to say, on the one hand, there is a refund of overpaid prices, on the other hand, but also of lost profits that arise when a company has lost demand through a cartel. The law lays down the right to disclose evidence so that they can then also be used in civil law proceedings. It regulates the binding effect of competition decisions on civil claims for damages. This means that even if the procedure has already been carried out, the civil complainant can rely on it. It harmonises the limitation period; it will be for five years ............................................................................................................................................................................................................................................................... It controls when it can start. The limitation begins only after the damage cartel has been dissolved and is interrupted when the investigations begin. It should be stressed in particular that a total liability is held by all parties involved. That's to say, everyone in the cartel is in solidarity. The damage only needs to be paid once, but if a cartel member has gone bankrupt in the meantime, the others come to the handcushion and have to pay accordingly. It must be mentioned that this does not apply to small businesses, to SMEs. The damage will only be paid to direct customers. An important point, among the exceptions to this solidarity, is also the leniency regulation. Chandlers are important to cover cartels at all. On the other hand, it can't be that someone who first enriches himself and then makes an advertisement doesn't need to fear any more civil consequences. It is now made clear for the first time that there is a limitation of liability that the declarant is only used for its enrichment to the extent of liability, has no fines and has no right to be feared by other antitrust members, as it is accordingly published and cannot be brought against it. This law will provide more transparency through the publication of decisions. There is an improvement in enforcement, as external drives can now be seized, for example. And there is also an endowment of the fines for the Federal Competition Authority and for the VKI. In order to better combat cartels, illegal cartels. The damage caused by cartels can be enormous in terms of the economy. This law, which implements the directive, should avoid these damage as far as possible. It is also worth mentioning that we have adhered to the directive. There has been no overarching Austrian additional liability potential, and we have not operated any gold plating. That is why this directive has been implemented as far as possible, and I thank you for it. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2017-03-30-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00173_d7e7391	Mr President. Federal Minister! Appreciate spectators here and at home! Ladies and gentlemen in the House! The agenda today includes a government template to amend the Cartel Act 2005, the Competition Act and the Federal Act to improve local supply and conditions of competition and to transfer it to the new Cartel and Competition Amendment Act 2017. My previous speaker has already spoken in great detail about this; I would like to present the key points again and summarize them. The purpose of this Act is to create legal certainty and claims for damages in the event of infringements of competition law. Furthermore, this law aims to improve transparency in antitrust proceedings, to ensure the quality of expert advice in cartel proceedings, to ensure fair competition in the supply chain and further modernisation measures. The law is an EU directive to be transposed into national law. A reform of Austrian antitrust law is also included in the Government Convention. Achieving the goal of creating fair rules for competition should, among other things, increase transparency in the cartel procedure – for example, by naming after a procedure has been completed – and in the settlement. Furthermore, successful procedure programmes will be ensured and the limitation provisions adjusted. On the part of the SPÖ Group, we naturally support all the amendments to a law that ensure greater legal certainty, transparency and fairness. That is why we will also agree to the new antitrust and competition law 2017 and request broad support from this law.

In most cases, the individual consumer is limited to very small amounts and only to a large amount for all. This often makes the cartels get away cheap. However, the EU Commission does not prevent the Federal Government from perhaps bringing its own law into line. Private initiatives, as recently announced by a former VKI employee, are well meant, but cannot replace a well-founded legal regime. In Germany, there is the success model of the German monopoly commission, in which representatives of science sit inside. Perhaps one could consider such a thing for Austria too; it is a success model that one can consider in Austria could be introduced, and perhaps it would be time for that. ParlaMint-AT_2017-03-30-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00173_d7e7503	Next, Mr Magistrate. Nice to speak. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2017-03-30-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00173_d7e7525	Mr President. Mr. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! This item also covers the implementation of the BRIS Directive in relation to the link between central, commercial and social registers. This is an important step, according to EU standards, up to 8. ==References====External links== The aim of this measure is to create cross-border access to business information through the European judicial portal. In certain cases, it is planned to establish automated communication between the national registry authorities of the Member States through a central European platform. The purpose of this proposal is to implement this Directive. In particular, it is necessary to amend the company's book law, to amend the EU Merger Act and the Law on Court Fees. In order to comply with this implementation obligation, it is proposed that the company book via the central European platform be made available on a legal basis, primarily in the new § 37 of the Company Book Act. The questioning of company information through the European judicial portal improves the access of businesses, consumers and authorities to this information. The automated mutual understanding of the national business registers in certain cases, e.g. in the case of mergers, is also expected to improve the quality of the data stored in the national registers. In this sense, it is a very, very sensible legislative proposal, and I ask for this proposal to be adopted. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2017-03-30-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00173_d7e7892	Mrs Dr Karl came to speak. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2017-03-30-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00173_d7e7913	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! This Federal Act, which amends the Law on the Law on the Law on the Law of the Law on the Law of the Law of the Law of the Justice, contains a number of measures which, on the one hand, contribute to the simplification and modernisation of the Court of Justice and, on the other hand, adapt the legal requirements to the factual requirements in the field of judicial administration, while at the same time providing for the clarifications that are urgently required. It is clarified, inter alia, that security measures can be taken in official buildings on special occasions even if they are not explicitly mentioned in the house rules. Another clarification concerns the clear assignment of the judicial departments to the judges appointed by a court. Each Judge and any Judge appointed or otherwise employed by a Court of First Instance shall, if necessary, be entitled to open his own judicial department. In other words, In principle, the number of judicial departments is no longer determined by the number of systematic planning posts, but by the number of judges actually used in the respective court. This corresponds to an urgent desire from practice. Practical needs are also met with regard to the competence for business distribution changes. The external Senates of the Oberlandesgerichts can best assess which explosive judge or which explosive judge or which representative judge is to be used in the specific case. It is therefore useful for the external senate to determine the place, extent and duration of the mission in the posting decision and to make the initial change of the business distribution in the operational court. On the other hand, it is in principle the competence of the staff Senate of the Court of First Instance to have the allocated resources and to respond adequately from the allocation decision to further developments at the court concerned, for example by changing the distribution of business. It should also be mentioned that a judicial administration ratio is also provided for the District Courts. It is intended to be determined solely according to objective criteria, based on the number of judges. Furthermore, the efficiency of electronic file management is to be optimised. In order to further reduce the number of physical documents, decision-making bodies will be able to transfer the verification of a signature to the office for physical input. This means that inputs no longer need to be submitted in paper form to the decision-making body before they can be electronically recorded. In conclusion, it should be noted that all proposed rules do not affect the fundamental aspects of the judicial organisation and remain without budgetary impact. Thank you.

I say quite frankly to you: I am still a little under the impression that yesterday in Engerau – this is a district of Bratislava – I, together with the Minister of Justice of Slovakia and the Minister of Justice of Hungary, have revealed a commemorative board to remind of Engerau's massacre. There was a camp for Jewish fellow citizens from Hungary, which was dissolved just before the end of the war. There were mass shootings and terrible things there. There were then the contemporary interesting and famous Engerau processes here in Vienna. For me, it was a very strong experience to see that three ministers of justice – from Slovakia, Hungary and Austria, who do not always share God’s view – feel connected and agree on the need for this kind of culture of memory. Especially the case of Engerau has shown how important it is to understand which mechanisms could lead to the fact that previously completely inconspicuous fellow citizens became mass murderers, and what actually had to happen. ParlaMint-AT_2017-03-30-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00173_d7e9160	Mr. Federal Minister! Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Package travel law – this is the implementation of a directive that has been implemented quite precisely, but that does not mean that we are happy here in Austria, because if a directive does not make sense, one should first of all resist it, not simply and simply take note of it and implement this directive. What is positive about this law? – There has so far been a possibility of overcompensation. That means: For example, if a flight had been delayed for several hours, one had to claim, on the one hand, to the tour operator and on the other due to the air passenger rights regulation. This is now clear: There's no overcompensation anymore. - So far, so positive. However, it is negative that this directive still has an evaluation horizon of about three years. This means that there may be changes in this situation. Come on. Today, however, we are adopting a law that has consequences for the tour operators – and then this law may have to be changed again in a few years; probably before there are high court decisions. That is why this is a bit a bad situation for the tour operators, because they do not have great legal certainty. But what is the essence of this law? – Nothing has changed for the major tour operators. For the – for example for TUI, the traffic office Ruefa – the status quo has remained unchanged, for which nothing changes. However, the small travel agencies will get massive problems, and it is assumed that they will simply and simply throw the towel sooner or later, because they have strongly expanded liability under this law. In particular, it is a question of redefining the concept of package travel. A package trip can now also be made in such a way that not already predetermined links of several travel services come together, but that only results later, and if there is such a package trip, then the tour operator has an extended liability. This begins once with the information about it and continues, and so it can happen that it is a package trip with which the tour operator – the small travel agency – did not expect and therefore perhaps did not make the information accordingly, and then he is fully liable for it. Therefore, it is assumed that small travel agencies are simply and simply no longer coming along and then coming under the wheels. That is the content of this law, which is the content of the directive. It will already be the case that the major tour operators have prevailed at EU level. The fact is, however, that the small travel agencies in Austria come under the wheels. It is now also mandatory to complete insolvency insurance and some more, which has a great effort, a bureaucratic effort and also a great financial effort, all of this, as I said, with the great risk of liability, so that the small tour operators are pushed to the brink. We of the FPÖ have no pleasure with this law, because we are very happy about every entrepreneur there is, especially about the small entrepreneurs, many of whom are active as a tour operator or travel agency – so far, I say – and therefore we reject this law.

This is in our view and also in the interest of the tourism industry, because it provides this legal certainty, which is important for it, and also enables it to take a practical approach.

While the old package travel directive from 1990 was enshrined in the Consumer Protection Act, it has now been completely resolved and this own regulation has been made out of the directive. I would like to highlight some positive aspects, namely more uniform European rules, which apply to travel that is booked either in the travel agency or on the Internet. Consumers are protected when suppliers go into bankruptcy; that was not the case until now. Liabilities for all services included in the package will be improved. We all know such journeys that you have collected individually, in which you had no security in advance. Now these travel blocks are regarded as a package trip, so there is also this safety. Customers and customers have a single contact person, they do not need to contact five different places. The cancellation conditions for these individual travel blocks are better. So I think, in the sense of consumers and people who like to travel, that is a positive aspect and a good law.

ParlaMint-AT_2017-03-30-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00173_d7e9500	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The package travel guidelines need to be revised over and over again because they also have to adapt to the conditions of our time, for example to the development of online sales of travel services. It is also time for our package travel law to change. However, the proposed amendment does not fully strengthen consumer protection, as it has been claimed. New compared to the previous package travel directive is the introduction of the so-called related travel services. Such a connected travel service must be assumed when a flight is booked online and then a rental car is rented via a link at a car rental company. If there is a related travel service, the company only has to state that there is no comprehensive protection under the package travel law. In the case of this travel service, each service provider shall be liable only for the provision of the travel services agreed in the travel contract. De facto, there is a problem that the package travel law should not really correspond to the reality of life of consumers, because many people organize the trip up to the flight on site. If there are any shortcomings in travel, then, of course, massive legal protection deficits may arise with regard to the enforcement of possible claims for damages. Of course, the question of who can claim the claim or where the court will be. There is a regulation in the distance and away business law that protects consumers on the Internet from overrun purchase decisions, so it is not understandable for me and for us why there is no such withdrawal rights for travel services here. In principle, of course, we welcome the amendment that will be adopted today, but I wish to continue to strengthen consumer protection and hope that we will work together on it. – Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2017-03-30-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00173_d7e9625	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The National Council is discussing and deciding today on a new package travel law. However – and here the law is both consumer-friendly and business-friendly –: These provisions apply not only to consumers, but also to travel agencies who can refuse travel under such circumstances without having to pay compensation. That is to say, under the title of almost higher violence, the law clearly expresses a consumer kindness as well as an economic kindness. I like that. New is the obligation to provide information, since it is the word ‘pre-contractual information’. There will be a standard information sheet in this regard, in which the following points will have to be made clear to the consumer to whom the traveler has to be communicated: These are essentially the tour management, the total price, the payment arrangements, the minimum number of participants due and information on the right of withdrawal of the traveler. I think that is very important information. The industry is already doing this properly, but now everyone has to do it by law – and that is a good thing. The new package travel law creates more clarity, it strengthens consumer protection. And since vacation trips are often abroad or are also offered to foreigners in Austria, it is simply correct and desirable that there is an EU-wide clear system. In this sense, the law is very good, although it is a clear move between economic and consumer protection. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2017-03-30-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00173_d7e9845	Mr President. Minister! High house! We are discussing among these three agenda items two related to civil law and one related to criminal law. This Additional Protocol largely incorporates provisions already adopted at European Union level in the Convention on Legal Assistance in Criminal Matters between the Member States of the European Union and complements the First Additional Protocol, no, if it is the Second Additional Protocol. The main provisions are as follows: Legal assistance also in the case of administrative infringements relating to the direct movement of authorities between the authorities responsible for the position and for the completion of requests for assistance; exchange of information; hearing of witnesses, experts and accused persons by video conference; hearing of witnesses and experts by telephone conference; rules on the establishment and use of joint investigation groups; mutual assistance to the Contracting States by the use of hidden investigators; the implementation of controlled supplies of Prohibitions and cross-border observation. As we have already implemented these regulations within the European Union, we do not need to put in place any additional technical measures, so this additional protocol does not impose any additional costs that we must bear. [[President Hofer shall take over the chairmanship.]] In principle – if I may add a few considerations – it is about the criminal integration of Europe. I remember a famous case from the time we were not yet in the European Union, a murder which was not committed in Austria but in the Indian Ocean. At that time, there were six dead and six attempted murder. The immediate perpetrator has been sentenced to life-long imprisonment in Austria, but his compliment has gone to Germany – he was a German – and has received a much lower penalty there. Similarly, the case concerning a bank in the southeastern Austria, when the one who cleared the bank – also a German – went to Germany and received a relatively low penalty, but the bank director here in Austria has almost received the highest penalty. With access to Europe, with this combination, we must also be careful not to pick up raisins by criminal law, so that not everyone can choose the country in which he receives the lowest penalty. We have put in place a variety of measures in Europe – you may remember the European arrest warrant that exists – in order to achieve a certain degree of harmonisation on our continent. A common Europe also includes harmonised access to criminal law, whether it be judicial criminal law or administrative criminal law. ParlaMint-AT_2017-03-30-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00173_d7e9931	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! I would like to comment on cross-border child abduction. It is always said that one parent abducts the child against the will of the other parent, brings it out of the country or then holds it in that country. This is a bad thing because they hit the most children, who may then become depressed, have emotional mood swings, so to speak, damage to life, and that is to prevent it. 60 such cases occur annually in Austria, and therefore the main concern is to protect children; To protect children from negative consequences, to protect against disease. In this respect, there is an international agreement, the Hague Convention on Civil Aspects of International Child Abduction. This is a multilateral treaty, signed by Austria and many others, and today it is about ten other countries becoming members as well; and only when the State concerned is a member and this regulation is thus fulfilled can children who are abducted there be brought back home. The most common causes of such child abductions are that in the case of a separation, divorce is tried to take advantage of it, or that the other parent should be harmed, or that the right to visit should be refused. So there are a variety of reasons, but all of them end up in the fact that the child is massively affected. The Hague Convention is intended to set up and safeguard the interests of children and ensure that children can return and grow up in the usual environment, a normal development can take place. That is the intention of this Convention, and that is why we must support it. It is important that this agreement be extended. Some critics say that this agreement would be toothless because no sanctions are associated with it. Nevertheless, it should be noted that there is an international legal, intergovernmental framework, which always depends on how much a state is doing to protect children. In any case, we are in favour and welcome the extension of this agreement. Thank you very much.

I may mention a few numbers: Since 1997, a total of 40,000 people with the professional maturity test have picked up Matura, and every year about 3,000 to 3,500 test subjects are taking part. ParlaMint-AT_2017-03-30-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00173_d7e11120	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! As a regional Member of Parliament, I should like to comment briefly on an agreement between the Republic of Austria and the Principality of Liechtenstein, which relates to equality in the field of maturity and higher education. ==References== There is already such an agreement on December 1997. As we all know, in the meantime in the higher education landscape of our two Contracting States, a number of amendments, and therefore the text of this Agreement is more than just adaptable and worthy and necessary. That is why we are taking this step today, and I know, because of numerous contacts, also with students from three countries in this region – Vorarlberg, Switzerland and Liechtenstein – that it is a long-standing desire to implement these simplifications. Important parts of the agreement are: first the mutual recognition of the certificates of maturity, second, the tuition fees or higher education taxes are the same as for own citizens. Thirdly, the recognition of studies and examinations is based on the ECTS, fourthly, it includes the recognition of diplomas in further studies and, finally, the right to conduct academic degrees in the respective other State. In the Committee, Mr Fekter pointed out that professional maturity examinations should also be included in such a scheme in the future. I believe that this suggestion is not only right but also important. It would be important to take these into account in the future. Wherever it is possible, you should actually implement it. - Thank you.

No Austrian or any Austrian would say that he or she is a animal torturer – all say they are animal protectors. Let me quote – in some points it has already been mentioned today – what we are implementing with this Animal Protection Act. There is, for example, a ban on dog neckbands with tensile mechanisms that could make breathing difficult for dogs, because it was always possible to strangle the dogs with so-called collars. We have a ban on the suspension of farmed wildlife that are not viable – a massive demand from animal welfare organisations. We have a ban on Internet trade for private people, because there was very, very much abuse with dog puppies and young cats. We have now incorporated the dog trainers qualified for animal protection into the law and the institutions that examine them. We have made it clearer by means of today's amendment. We have formulated a new and very good definition of animal shelters, animal pensions, animal asylenes and mercies, because it is necessary and important to define them properly by law, so that there are no problems when these animals take up. We have made a better definition of breeding by using a formulation of the organization Four paws and the animal protection ombudsmen who do so – I go in detail afterwards. We have done a removal of the permission of coral collars in service dogs. We have included in this law the acquisition of auxiliary persons who may be used for an anaesthesia in the future and the regulation concerning the type and proof of the subject matter. We have included in this law a ban on dyeing and tattooing for commercial or aesthetic reasons. In principle, we have better regulated Internet trade with animals. We have agreed that there will be no delivery of animals to young people under the age of 16. We have made it clear what is no bonding of dogs, for under this title, dog owners were shown, for example, when they walked with their dog on the leash, because anyone believed it was a bonding. This too has now been clearly regulated. We have created a new and clear regulation of the legal personality of the department for animal health and animal welfare as well as the possibility of transferring it to further tasks, for example, that it will become the contact point for animal welfare in the case of slaughter and animal welfare during transport. We have regulated a recording of the chip duty for uncastrated cats with release and also registration in a database. We have made it clear that not only commercial but all economic animal husbandries need authorisation. We have included in this law a regulation and reporting obligation for facilities that include, pass on or communicate animals. And what's very important: We have achieved an improvement in the legal status of the animal welfare ombudsmen through the possibility of a review survey at the Administrative Court and file inspection of the criminal courts in the case of animal welfare offences. When did I announce that? – I immediately announced with measures 2015/2016, with the start of the 2016 tax reform, that the cold progression will be abolished. Since then, I have repeatedly pointed out that we have to do this and that we have to implement it. However, I have also pointed out that we have gotten air through the enormous tariff relief we have made in 2016. Of course, the government programme has also agreed that we will do this in the first half of this year, and that will happen. How does the model that currently exists look like? – It is an automatic adjustment. That's the big difference, for example, when you look at Germany. We have the opportunity to suspend the adjustment if there is an economic crisis, and we have defined an adjustment that will then intervene if the aggregated value reaches at least 5 percent. However, if the value is then 6 per cent or 6.5 per cent, this value will of course be adjusted with this value and not with 5 per cent. So just take the situation, we collect three years of inflation, three years to 2% are 6 percent, then after the third year the rate is adjusted by 6 percent. Why you, Mr Klubobmann, are not right in the explanatory statement, then perhaps Mr Matznetter, who has already made an interlocutory call, will be present again. For the income classes you mentioned, the cold progression does not enter into force at all, because even if I adjust the lowest rate to 100 percent above that value, then everyone grows in it. [[Abg. - Oh, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no,]] And therefore, at a value of €1,200, a tax progression of a few euros is likely to occur. [[Abg. Stroll: But the discharge is degressive, you understand!]] – Of course, and I'll be right back. The Federal Government's work programme has therefore said that we are dealing with this issue of cold progression. We are currently negotiating this compromise proposal. By the way, the 100 percent adjustment of the first two stages is calculated on all levels, provided that the progressive control system is correctly understood. [[Abg. Stroll: From the third level!]] The adaptation to this depends on the Progression Report. That is to say, the automatic adjustment takes place first, and the rest is also distributed, but after a progression report, in which experts determine – I am referring to the Progression Report – whether there is a level of taxation in which progression has been particularly severe. This also makes Germany with this progress report. We have determined that we want to make about 80 percent automatically and 20 percent about this progression report. Once again, I expressly hold on – and in doing so, I never said anything else –: The cold progression is a gift from citizens to the state and not a gift from the federal government to the citizens. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] That is why this draft now before us does exactly that: that this cold progression is no longer taking place where the inflation surcharge is taking place. I assume, ladies and gentlemen, that the complexity of the questions has been inhaled by all of you, that in the meantime you all know what a basic model, an inflation model and a indicative model is, so I will not answer the questions to the question itself. Now I come to answer your questions. On Question 2: The question is about income-related taxes. But since, Mr Klubobmann Strolz, the corporate tax is such a tax, I assume that you have only seen income-related taxes outside the corporation tax, because the corporate tax is not covered by the cold progression due to the rates. If you are now exclusively addressing wage and income tax – you see only this sphere – it is to be said that the proportion of the increase in income, which goes back to the cold progression, is naturally very much dependent on inflation compared to the previous years. In other words, the proportion of cold progression – that is, the normal yield – has been between 40 percent and 90 percent in recent years. Depending on how much the respective rate is applied, the split is as high as possible. On Question 4: The cumulative increase in revenue from the cold progression between the tax reforms 2009 and 2016 – and now the point I have announced – totals around 10 billion euros. With the 2009 tax reform, an annual tax relief of €3 billion was implemented in the area of wage and income tax, and with the 2016 tax reform, a volume of more than €5 billion per year and not cumulatively charged. It is clear that overcompensation of cold progression in these years has been achieved through the two stages of tax reform. So if you are saying here that, I do not know exactly what you are always using, you would have taken the other person into your pocket, then it may be possible to vote temporarily from your point of view, but not by implementing the two levels of tax reform, because they have led to the tax reform overcompensating the cold progression. On question 6: Estimates of future expected tax revenue with or without cold progression are clearly insecure. This also applies to inflation forecasts. From the current point of view, it is simply not yet possible to estimate the extent to which exactly the most recent tariff adjustment would take place, let alone how the associated effects will be. I would like to draw your attention once again to the fact that we have high levels of variation. I remember that we had an inflation rate of 0.8 per cent, now we have an inflation rate of 1.8 per cent, and in this rate of variation it is moving. That is why this cannot be answered today. Questions 7 and 8: With reference to the €200 million per percentage point of inflation per year, the result is an increase in revenue for the countries of around €42 million and for the municipalities of around €24 million. On Question 9: I see no contradiction in the budget report here, because even weaker growth means an increase over the starting point. As a general rule, wages are also higher than inflationary trends, so there will be a higher rate of wage tax compared with the previous year even if the dynamics are slightly reduced. Moreover, as regards the link between wage tax revenue and cold progression: Cold progression always occurs in the area of wage tax only when the total wage and salary increases and at the same time there is an increase in the price. Therefore, a weaker growth in the total wage and salary rate leads to a relatively weaker growth of the corresponding tax revenue and, if there is inflation, cold progression. On Question 10: Unfortunately, the request does not mention what kind of cost is meant. The following is considered to be a reduction in gross domestic product. First of all, it should be pointed out that in 2009 and 2016 there were the massive discharges I have already quoted in relation to the tax rate. These also had the purpose of counteracting the cold progression. Tax reform 2015/2016 even went far beyond the compensation of cold progression. In total, it is therefore to be said that the discharges made in 2009 and 2016 do not assume that the cold progression has had a negative impact on the work incentives. Empirical evidence for the relationship between cold progression and work incentives we have no at all. Questions 11 to 16: First of all, I would like to answer your question, which is justified by the decisions you take here in Parliament, because tax laws are indeed passed by you. So our actions are based on this now valid legal basis. I also think that the tax relief agreed with the 2016 tax reform was economically necessary. This does not mean, however, that an economic necessity does not arise over time. That is why I am in favour of taking measures against cold progression. I have discussed with experts the advantages and disadvantages of different models. I have also stated in the opening statement that I am in principle committed to the automatic linear adjustment and relief of tax rates. A compromise proposal has now been drawn up within the framework of the Federal Government's work programme. The key points of the Federal Government's work programme are known. Everything else is currently the subject of negotiations. To your question, how long will that take, I have to say: It doesn't depend on me alone. My proposal has been on the table for a few weeks now as a legislative text and a draft, and we had another round of talks last week with the aim of solving this problem and submitting it to Parliament. As regards the ongoing negotiations with the coalition partner, it is currently planned that we will be able to send the review to the end of April. It could therefore go very quickly from my point of view, and this could be decided in the National Council in the first half of the year, if the review was carried out accordingly. In financing such a measure, I stick to my basic principle that we do not need new taxes. I am therefore in favour of fiscal consolidation as before. The details are presented in the framework of the Federal Finance Act and the Federal Financial Framework Act in autumn. Questions 17 to 23: Of course, we have analysed the models you have mentioned for their advantages and disadvantages. Based on the experience of this analysis, we have designed a model that seems best suited to our system, in any case according to our view. This is also based on experience in other areas, for example, explicitly cited, in lease contracts where there are such collective clauses that the rent will not be changed until a certain threshold in inflation is exceeded. In addition, the intention of the model is that there is a noticeable relief. This is guaranteed only if adjustments are not made annually. Take another example of the last inflation: 0.8 percent adjusted to € 11 000. You can calculate yourself relatively quickly: No one's gonna notice that. At 5 per cent or 6 per cent, however, this will immediately strike accordingly. Let me therefore briefly give an overview of the models discussed in Switzerland and Sweden. Switzerland also has a progressive tax system and collects both local, cantonal and federal taxes. Since 2011, tariffs and tax deductions have largely been automatically adjusted to the price trend, according to the national index of consumer prices. This applies both at federal and cantonal level. The annual adjustment at the federal level is carried out automatically and is constitutionally anchored. At local level, the adjustment is usually also made annually and automatically, but de facto the adaptation in many cantons is different. Why doesn't the model take us? - Because we don't have taxes at the local level. The Swedish model also quoted: Swedish income tax is also abolished at both local and federal level. So this too is a model that would not grasp us. Unlike in Switzerland – which has already been quoted – there is an adjustment beyond price trends in the field of real wage increases. Now to the individual models: The inflation model usually provides for an annual adjustment. This, in my view, has the disadvantage that this is complicated because annual adjustment and change of tariff levels are associated with it. With the fixed model, the adjustment is made by a fixed percentage. The disadvantage from my point of view is that this lacks an economic basis. The nominal wage model, which corresponds to the Swedish model, is adjusted to the extent of inflation plus 2 percent. Disadvantage: There is no basis for adapting beyond inflation. If we look at further details of the models, I think that the framework of this urgent request would be going on, but they will certainly be still to be discussed. On questions 24 to 28: As has already been pointed out several times, the precise design – including the Progression Report – is the subject of the negotiations currently under way. The key points of the report are: The starting point for the Progression Report on tariff levels 3 to 6 is in particular the last available consumer surveys of Statistics Austria and the last available data from EU statistics on income and living conditions of Statistics Austria. It is also important for me to include external experts in this progress report. I therefore also propose in the proposal that an economic research institute be commissioned to calculate these values. The discharge of the upper levels of tariffs on the basis of the Progression Report should be determined by means of a regulation, including referral to the Council of Ministers. It is not intended to be allowed, but a report is to be submitted, which will then be decided. Only in this way can it be ensured that a discharge is also made quickly and unbureaucratically in this area. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, the accusation of Klubobmann Strolz, we would have promised something and not delivered, goes into emptiness. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Stroll]] We delivered the model properly. You can only appeal to what I promised. I said the model will be delivered. We have determined within the framework of the government programme that we will do so. Do not insure people! This government has undertaken the project, it is clearly stated that we are working on the project. Therefore, thank you once again for the opportunity to present this position in the context of a urgent request. I mentioned earlier: Sometimes it's not that pleasant. Today it was wonderful.

It is therefore important that we make an adjustment to the existing 1999 Treaty by means of this Police Cooperation Treaty. This Police Cooperation Treaty also provides for higher requirements for police cooperation, or it is also necessary to respond to the Schengen participation of Switzerland, which joined Schengen 2008, and Liechtenstein, which joined in 2011. One of the most important innovations to this Treaty is that hidden investigations are greatly facilitated for the purpose of preventing criminal offences capable of delivery. There is also a marked improvement in cross-border action in the territory of the other Contracting State without prior consent in the event of a direct significant risk to body, life and property. This also applies to cooperation in the protection of witnesses and victims, to support in return, police transit and also to the transfer of persons at the State border. Cooperation in the cross-border prosecution of traffic offences will also be improved. However, it was necessary that an implementing agreement for this area was also made. ==References== ==References== Unfortunately, it was then a little delayed due to data protection concerns of the Federal Chancellor's Office, but these concerns could now be eliminated. To this end, it can be said that the approximately five million speed crossings in Austria involve around one million wheelbars with foreign vehicle registration. That is why it is necessary to make possible a follow-up to that effect. Traffic safety requires law enforcement of foreigners as well as of domestic authors. The Police Cooperation Treaty includes provisions on the cross-border prosecution of traffic offences, i.e. automated vehicle registration data exchange, investigation and hearing of the handlebar, transmission and delivery of official documents and also for enforcement assistance. All in all, this is an important contract that will improve security in the triangle. Thank you.

In 2005, 11 states signed the so-called Prüm Treaty, sometimes also called the Schengen III Treaty. The purpose of this Treaty is to improve the cross-border prevention and prosecution of crimes. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! As the day-to-day practice shows, the 1999 Treaty is still a very good basis for close police, border police cooperation on security and crime. Fighting. Nevertheless, ladies and gentlemen, it is time, after 18 years, to review and modernise the Treaty. For example, the accession of Switzerland and Liechtenstein to the Schengen area in 2008 and 2011 significantly changed the framework conditions for cross-border work. In practice, it has also been shown that all three countries now consider enlargement and concreteisation of powers to be useful in some areas. In particular, this concerns areas such as the protection of witnesses and victims; what is particularly important to me is the area of transport regulations and the violations of road traffic regulations and the intervention in emergencies. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! After nearly two decades, this trilateral cross-border cooperation between the security and customs authorities from Switzerland, Liechtenstein and Austria can rightly be described as a success. It is precisely in this direction that our motion for a resolution, in which we call for the creation of accessible access to information, in which we take up this issue and try to contribute a bit to a solution. There is an OECD study from the years 2011/2012, unfortunately no newer one showing that there are around 960 000 adults in Austria who cannot read well, who cannot read meaningfully or have problems reading more complex texts. These are about 15 percent of the adult population, who can only understand short texts on well-known topics. This number of people is composed of different groups of people. For example, it is people with learning disabilities, people with low schooling, people with non-German mother tongue, people with visual impairment, hearing impairment and also with motor disability. It is not just people with disabilities, it is also about migrants, asylum seekers and asylum seekers who do not or do not understand our language. These people, however, are very often involved with authorities and, of course, also with the Ministry of the Interior. That is why we see it as very important that important information from the Authority be provided in the lighter-reading version, because it certainly makes a good and essential contribution to the search for information for self-reliance for all people. There are already documents in the easy-to-read version, but there are only a few; for example, there is information about the tasks of the Federal Criminal Office. I have looked at this and have to say that it is really very understandable and comprehensible that this is easy to read for people who are hard to read. But especially in the field of administration there are numerous information sheets, brochures, forms you need and the affected also to teach about their rights and duties. And it is particularly important that these documents be presented in a slightly legible form, so misunderstandings can be avoided as far as possible from the outset. Finally, I would also like to thank all the political groups for their support for our request. Thank you very much.

(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Today we are adopting the motion for a resolution on ‘accessible access to information’, that is, the lighter reading version, and I would like to address a few words to you. With this resolution, one would like to work towards the implementation of the lighter-reading version and the accessibility of access to information, and to make it possible for people to communicate freely. I would like to mention that the UN Convention in Article 9 says that all Contracting States are obliged to ensure barrier-free communication. We are once again at a point where we want to discuss whether we should be able to do so. There is a commitment to this! Austria has prepared the National Action Plan with 250 measures, and in these 250 measures there are other forms of communication. For example, it is said that one definitely needs to focus more on the lighter reading version in texts, but not only in one ministry, but in all. An analysis has shown that until now only six Ministries have dealt with the subject of lighter reading, i.e. only six Ministries have taken this initiative. The fact is that all ministries are obliged to ensure accessibility of access to information and to implement it. The previous speakers have already said that the lighter reading version, i.e. the easier understanding of texts, is conducive not only to people with learning disabilities, but to all people. But often the lighter-reading version is not considered professional, but you want to sell yourself outside as a professional. Nevertheless, it is true that an easy-to-understand language, an easy understanding for each one, is a goal-leading one. The accessibility, barrier-free communication is not only limited to the lighter-reading version, but we also speak here of the Braille script, of tactile communication, of various sign languages – an area is the lighter-reading version. And that, which we are voting on today and which we will also agree with, is a small aspect of the diversity of accessibility-free communication, to which we confess, and this confession is very, very important. I think it's a good step for us to move there, but I have to say, that's a very, very small step. There would have to be a very broad understanding that accessibility-free communication is beneficial for everyone. – Thank you very much. Good night!

ParlaMint-AT_2017-03-30-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00173_d7e23311	We're now on the 28th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mrs Lichtenecker is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2017-03-30-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00173_d7e23340	Madam President. Mr. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! In the end, the radio equipment market surveillance law is nothing but the implementation of an EU directive. The main objective is to avoid risks to health and radio disturbances in the use of radio frequencies. Finally, it is about the fact that changes to the software that change the wireless parameters are effectively prevented by technical blocking. The question now is: How is the law implemented? It has, of course, its advantages, as it is, but we believe that some problems will arise, and there are disadvantages that are already on the table. That is why we will not support it. I will mention some of these disadvantages: The first is that the necessary conformity assessments will ultimately be charged by alternative software and by independent software providers. This also means, of course, that there can be a corresponding disadvantage for start-ups in this area. Another negative consequence, of course, is that the restriction on alternative software is anti-innovation, which is why it is also to be rejected in this form. Another essential point is that projects with open source use will also be threatened in the future. These are sufficient reasons for us not to carry this matter.

ParlaMint-AT_2017-03-30-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00173_d7e23402	Mr. Minister! High house! We are now discussing the radio system market surveillance law. What's this about? – It is a question of uniformly regulating radio systems across Europe. It is particularly important for this scheme to ensure that the trade chain is comprehensible, i.e. that products coming from overseas to Europe are comprehensible to consumers: Who can they turn to? Who is responsible for the conformity of these products? Here is also a responsibility with the importer. What's this about? – Of course, on the issue that when radios are operated today – and the number of radios is increasing – it cannot be that users manipulate the radio frequency and signal strength. It is important that radio systems are aligned to signal strengths in Europe so that the users of such systems do not interfere with each other. And, of course, we still have an internal discussion, and we have also conducted it very intensively in the Committee, because of the size of these chips and because of the cost-effectiveness of these chips. situation some have the presumption: Aha, if there is a blocking of manipulation, it will no longer be possible to play another software in the future. We have clearly also stated in the explanations that this law clearly does not say that an alternative software cannot be played on these chips. And this discussion, which has now begun in the community, also leads to an increasing number of chip manufacturers, well-known chip manufacturers, who say that they will also offer users the opportunity to use open-source applications in the future, because there is once another user interface because there are improvements in the system, because of course we have the experience that such applications need to be handled very quickly and updates. That is to say, the objective, also of this manufacturer, renowned manufacturers, is to continue to ensure that there is this possibility. Of course, we cannot intervene in the market, we can only create the conditions. The framework conditions indicate that there will continue to be the possibility in the future, and one can also see that the debate in the Community is that this offer is actually being created. This is not just a phenomenon that affects Europe, it affects the USA as well. That is why I am sure that we will do something together here and that we will finally see that we really create practical applications here. There are also exceptions to this law so that if you want to present a new, innovative radio system at fairs today, you can put it into operation there, even if it does not yet comply with these guidelines. So it has also been ensured that this measure can also be implemented in practice. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2017-03-30-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00173_d7e23472	Madam President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! It has already been mentioned that this is the implementation of an EU directive, which also includes manufacturers and operators of radio stations. If one has looked around a little, especially in the preparation of the committee and now in favour of the plenary, it has become aware of how many devices are actually covered by this new directive. This starts with the mobile phone, which concerns a motion sensor, the car parking lot that locks or locks the car by radio, or, as the Minister has said today in an APA message, a WLAN doll or remote-controlled toys. All of this concerns this directive, and of course much more. What's this about? – It is about creating a uniform system for these installations. This concerns compliance with standards, but at the same time also the notification of this hardware and software, and of course it also concerns consumer protection, but also, in the sense of fair competition, a uniform appeal procedure and also the possibility of giving warnings to the competent Minister, for example in the case of recall actions or in the case of sales restrictions. I would also like to refer to the opinions that have been submitted. It is clear that it is a directive which is to be transposed, and that is equally what is being done here, but it is also sensible. The concern expressed in the opinions is that, as Mr Lichtenecker has already mentioned, the use of open-source software, the use of freely accessible software in the field of various devices, is becoming more difficult, especially in the case of Internet providers. From my point of view, this is a very important issue. The open-source community, the open-source software provides enormous potential on the free market. It is a carrier of innovation, and also in Austria this community has an insane potential. However, Mr Vogl has already stated that this has been included in the explanations, both in the EU Directive and in our legislative text. One wish, which has also been expressed, is that, in the course of the adoption of regulations to which the Minister is authorised, account should also be taken of the open-source community and, of course, the desire to also enable them in the conformity assessment, e.g. in the case of open-source software related to a particular device. I should also like to ask the Minister that this be taken into account in the adoption of regulations. Another aspect, which Mr Kucher and I have also brought into the committee, with an amendment or with a committee statement, concerns the cross-border use of, for example, mobile and data contracts. EU law requires providers to inform the user, if the data volume is exceeded or when additional costs are incurred. For example, if you are on the move with the tablet, the problem is that the SMS function will not be activated. And in order to ensure that not only the providers, but also the equipment manufacturers, are taken into account, we have tabled this amendment to clarify that this function must also be ensured. So it's a good law, and I'm asking for your support.

Cuckoo.]] I think that other committees could cut themselves off and see how this works in the FIT committee. Our party chairman always says politics is the place where we make up as we do live together. – Yes, but politics is not the place where funding for applied research should be decided. The FFG is an internationally renowned organization that repeatedly proves its professionalism and does excellent work, but in the structure of the FFG there is simply a beauty mistake: The Advisory Board for Basic Programmes is half-occupied with representatives of the social partners: Chamber of Commerce, Chamber of Workers, ÖGB, Trade Union for Private Workers, Chamber of Agriculture and so on. I would now like to exaggerate all the arguments that Mr Klug will make directly after me in advance, so that you can have a good decision-making basis. Such political proximity is simply unusual internationally and therefore, in our opinion, is also changing. This is not because we believe that policy in the FFG has basically lost nothing. The Republic is giving the money and is therefore also sitting in the Supervisory Board, which is perfectly okay. Neither, because we would believe that the sent members in the advisory board cannot be experts – that is not the reason. This has to be changed because it simply doesn't have to be and because there should be no doubt that the funding decisions are comprehensible to all the applicants, and because international experts could also be brought into such a body. For example, the Swiss Commission for Technology and Innovation could be considered as an example where decision-making bodies are composed of selected experts. There is a website where, for example, every commitment, as Swiss say, can be read for each individual. No matter which Supervisory Board they sit in, where they are directors, where they are members, all this is transparently comprehensible there. So it does not work for anyone unfairly or whatever when someone makes a request and this is rejected or allowed. But why do we actually do it in such a way that we bring the social partners in? – Not because it is in the law, because the law only states that the appointment of the advisory boards should take place after a balanced gender relationship. This is not the case at present, but I found it very positive that the Minister in the Committee has said that he will take care that this will be different in the future. That's important, because at least that's what the law says. But what we do voluntarily, without any legal basis, is to fulfil the social security rate. Where can you find out why this is so? – This is not regulated by the law, but, for example, in the FFG’s Internal Organisation Directive, it should be read that the Chamber of Commerce has a right to propose a majority of its members. What did the Committee then put forward for an argument? – The Advisory Board does not have any funding decisions at all, so this is not in the law, but it is only consultative. Not only that there is something different on the FFG website, but also in these internal organizational guidelines of the FFG you can read on page 5: ‘A technical decision is the recommendation to be made exclusively by the assessment body’ – that is the advisory board – ‘for basic programmes [...] to which the management of the FFG is in principle bound’. They are therefore very likely to make funding decisions, as is the case in the Organisational Directive of the FFG. Mr Klug, if you do not think so, I can print this text for you and put it down to you so that you can read it. It is not about imposing on the FFG that there would be political influence in support decisions, but it is about never giving anyone the chance to suspect that. There is such a thing there, for which depoliticization is there, for which there is transparency. It's about perceptibility, it's about international standards, it's about securing the excellent reputation of the FFG in the long run, and I'm a bit concerned about the principle.

I can also mention that the country of Burgenland is funding the annual ticket with 50 per cent, the ticket for the journey from Eisenstadt or Neusiedl by the lake to Vienna costs the counterparts €1,435, and the country of Burgenland is equally high. A subsidy for public transport at this level is almost unique. We see that public transport is of great value to the country of Burgenland and to those responsible for transport in Burgenland. Dear ladies and gentlemen! These figures speak for themselves. However, I would also like to remind you that the top youth ticket is a great offer for young people, students and students, with 60 € they are mobile within the entire area of the East-Region Transport Association. It also makes it possible for young people not to have to drive private cars on weekends and not to risk having too much alcohol in traffic controls. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen, all the best for the transport network! Madam President-in-Office of the Council, thank you again for the report.

ParlaMint-AT_2017-03-30-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00173_d7e25976	Madam President. President of the Court of Auditors! High house! Ladies and gentlemen! Court of Auditors Report TOP 31 to 33: I'm taking a position on TOP 31. Mr. Kucher, you said: I don't know what to say to the subways. I'm sure I'll tell you something about the subways: The financing of the Vienna metros, a federal agreement of 1979, was implemented only by means of a private law agreement. The Court of Auditors examines the Federal Government's financial contribution to the expansion of Vienna's U3 and U6 metro lines. In 1979, a 15a agreement on the allocation of costs of half was concluded. The Court of Auditors' report shows that the agreement was continued under private law from 1980 onwards, which is a circumvention of the rules of the financial constitution. Financial allocations have been made regularly without legal regulations, the National Council has been totally eliminated, and this is an intolerable state. It is interesting that four stages of expansion – Mr. Kucher, four stages of expansion! – be carried out, and only at the fifth stage of enlargement will Parliament be asked again and have to give its approval. This is a very interesting development, Madam President. That's why it's good that there's the Federal Court of Auditors who's checking these things. Thank you very much.
